\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{S}_{A}\in A^{s}\setminus A)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{Z}\in A^{s}\setminus A)+\big[\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{S}_{A}\in A^{s}\setminus A)-\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{Z}\in A^{s}\setminus A)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{Z}\in A^{s}\setminus A)+2\rho(\boldsymbol{S}_{A},\boldsymbol{Z})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{Z}\in A^{s}\setminus A)+2\rho(\boldsymbol{S}_{A},\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}})+2\rho(\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}},\boldsymbol{Z}).

We can proceed similarly to conclude that
\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{S}_{A}\in A\setminus A^{-s})\leq\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{Z}\in A\setminus A^{-s})+2\rho(\boldsymbol{S}_{A},\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}})+2\rho(\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}},\boldsymbol{Z})
. Since
\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{Z}\in A^{s}\setminus A)\lor\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{Z}\in A\setminus A^{-s})\leq 2\Delta(\boldsymbol{Z},s)
, we can take the supremum over
A\in\mathcal{R}
to write, for every
s>0
,

\displaystyle\rho(\boldsymbol{S}_{X},\boldsymbol{S}_{A})\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{B}\|_{\infty}>s)+2\Delta(\boldsymbol{Z},s)+2\rho(\boldsymbol{S}_{A},\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}})+2\rho(\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}},\boldsymbol{Z}).

By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{X},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{X},\boldsymbol{S}_{A}\right)+\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{A},\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}}\right)+\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}},\boldsymbol{Z}\right).

Finally, combine the last two displays we obtain the inequality

\displaystyle\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{X},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)+2\Delta(\boldsymbol{Z},s)+\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{B}\|_{\infty}>s\right)+3\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{A},\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}}\right).

(S.2)

We now proceed to bound each of the terms on the right-hand side.

Step 1.
Recall that
\{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{j}:j\in[m]\}
is a zero-mean independent sequence, so we deal with the first term in (
S.2
) applying Theorem 2.2 in
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov and
Koike 2020
in the form

\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{j}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\boldsymbol{W}_{j}:=\sqrt{\frac{m}{T}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{j},

which give us

\displaystyle\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log m\left[\frac{(\log d)M_{\Sigma}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\frac{(\log d)^{3/2}\sqrt{\mu_{4}}}{m\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\frac{(\mathcal{M}\log d)^{2}}{m\sigma_{*}^{2}}\right]

(S.3)

\displaystyle\qquad+\inf_{x>0}\left[\frac{\log d\sqrt{\log m\log(dm)H(x)}}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\frac{x(\log d)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}}\right],

where
M_{\Sigma}:=\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{S}_{X}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}}}\|_{\max}
,
\mu_{4}:=\max_{i\in[d]}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|W_{j,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{4}^{4}
,
\mathcal{M}:={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\max_{j,i}|W_{j,i}|\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{4}
and
H(x):=\max_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{E}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\|_{\infty}^{4}\mathds{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\|_{\infty}>x\}
for
x>0
.

From Lemma
S.2
we have, for
p\in[2,\infty)
and
j\in[m]
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{A}_{j,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|A_{j,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{A}_{j}}X_{t,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\lesssim\sqrt{a+b}\mathscr{A}_{a,p}L_{p}\lesssim\sqrt{a}\mathscr{A}_{a,p}L_{p}
where
L_{p}:=\max_{i\in[d]}\max_{t\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{t,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p+\epsilon}
,
W_{j,i}
and
X_{t,i}
denotes the
i
-th element of
\boldsymbol{W}_{j}
and
\boldsymbol{X}_{t}
respectively, for
i\in[d]
. Thus, for
i\in[d]
,
j\in[m]
and
p\in[2,\infty)
.

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|W_{j,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\lesssim L_{p}\mathscr{A}_{a,p}\sqrt{\frac{ma}{T}}\leq L_{p}\mathscr{A}_{a,p}=:R_{p}.

Expression (1,12a) and (1.12b) in
Rio 2017
give us, for
t,s\in[T]
,
i,k\in[d]
and
p\in[1,\infty]
,

\left|\mathbb{E}(X_{t,i}X_{s,k})\right|\leq 2\alpha_{|t-s|}^{1-2/(p+\epsilon)}\|X_{t,i}\|_{p+\epsilon}\|X_{t,k}\|_{p+\epsilon}\leq 2L_{p}^{2}\alpha_{|t-s|}^{1-2/p}.

Recall that
\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}}^{G}\sim N(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}}})
and
\boldsymbol{Z}\sim N(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{S}_{X}})
, where

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}}}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{A}_{j}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{A}_{j}}\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{s}^{\prime})\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{S}_{X}}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{s}^{\prime}).

Define
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\ell}:=\sum_{i=1}^{a-1}\sum_{j=1}^{a-1}\mathbb{E}(X_{i+\ell}X^{\prime}_{a+j+\ell})
for
\ell\geq 1
, then using the bound above we have

\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\ell}\|_{\max}\leq 2L_{p}^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{a-1}m\alpha_{m}^{1-2/p},\quad

Then,

\displaystyle M_{\Sigma}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{4m}{T}\max_{\ell}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\ell}\|_{\max}+\frac{m}{T}\max_{j\in[m]}\|\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{B}_{j}\boldsymbol{B}_{j}^{\prime})\|_{\max}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{|t-s|>a}\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{s}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L_{p}^{2}}{T}\left[m\mathscr{A}_{2}+mb\mathscr{A}_{1}(b)+T^{2}\alpha_{a}^{1-2/p}\right],

where
\mathscr{A}_{1}(m):=\sum_{n=0}^{m-1}\alpha_{n}^{1-2/p}
for
m\geq 1
and
\mathscr{A}_{2}:=\sum_{n=1}^{a-1}n\alpha_{n}^{1-2/p}
.

Also,

\displaystyle\mu_{4}

\displaystyle\leq m\max_{i\in[d]}\max_{j\in[m]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|W_{j,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{4}^{4}\lesssim mR_{4}^{4}

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\max_{j,i}|W_{j,i}|\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\leq\left(md\max_{j,i}|W_{j,i}|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\lesssim R_{p}(md)^{1/p}

\displaystyle H(x)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{E}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\|_{\infty}^{p}/x^{p-4}\lesssim R_{p}^{p}d/x^{p-4}

Plug these bounds back into
S.3
and set
x=\left[\frac{\log m\log(dm)R_{p}d}{\sigma_{*}^{2}\log d}\right]^{1/(p-2)}
to equate the terms inside the infimum. As a consequence, we are left with

\displaystyle\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L_{p}^{2}\left[m\mathscr{A}_{2}+mb\mathscr{A}_{1}(b)+T^{2}\alpha_{a}^{1-2/p}\right]\log m\log d}{T\sigma_{*}^{2}}

(S.4)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{R_{4}^{2}(\log d)^{3/2}\log m}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\frac{R_{p}^{2}(md)^{2/p}(\log d)^{2}\log m}{m\sigma_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\left[R_{p}d(\log d)^{(3/2)p-4}\log m\log(dm)\right]}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}^{p/(p-2)}}^{1/(p-2)}.

Step 2.
By Nazarov’s inequality for Gaussian random vectors (Theorem 1 in
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov and
Kato 2017
) we can bound the second term in (
S.2
) as

\Delta\left(\boldsymbol{Z},s\right)\lesssim s\sqrt{1\lor\log d}.

Similarly to Step 1, Lemma
S.2
give us for
j\in[m]
,
i\in[d]
and
p\in[2,\infty)

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{B}_{j,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|B_{j,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{j}}X_{t,k}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\lesssim\sqrt{b}\mathscr{A}_{b,p}L_{p},

where
B_{j,k}
and
N_{t,k}
denotes the
i
-th element of
\boldsymbol{B}_{j}
and
\boldsymbol{X}_{t}
respectively, for
i\in[d]
; and
\mathscr{A}_{b}:=\left[\sum_{0\leq m<b}(m+1)^{p/2-2}\alpha_{m}^{\epsilon/(p+\epsilon)}\right]^{\tfrac{2}{p}}
. We can then bound the third term in (
S.2
) by Markov’s inequality as

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{B}\|_{\infty}>s\right)\leq d\left(\frac{\sqrt{mb/T}\mathscr{A}_{b,p}L_{p}}{s}\right)^{p}.

We can set
s=s^{*}:=\big[d^{1/p}\sqrt{mb/T}\mathscr{A}_{b,p}L_{p}\big]^{p/(p+1)}
to equate (up to log terms) the two terms above containing
s
and obtain

\Delta\left(\boldsymbol{Z},s\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{B}\|_{\infty}>s\right)\lesssim\left(d^{1/p}\sqrt{\tfrac{mb}{T}}\mathscr{A}_{b,p}L_{p}\right)^{\tfrac{p}{p+1}}\sqrt{1\lor\log d}.

Step 3.
Notice that any measurable
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
we have
|\mathbb{P}[(\boldsymbol{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{A}_{2})\in A]-\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{1},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{2}\in A]|\leq\alpha_{b}
where
\{\alpha_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}\}
. Then, the last term in (
S.2
) can be upper bounded by
(m-1)\alpha_{b}
by induction, i.e,

\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{A},\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}}\right)\leq(m-1)\alpha_{b}.

Step 4.
Applying
the bound derived in the steps above back into (
S.2
) we are left with

\displaystyle\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{X},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L_{p}^{2}\left[m\mathscr{A}_{2}+mb\mathscr{A}_{1}(b)+T^{2}\alpha_{a}^{1-2/p}\right]\log m\log d}{T\sigma_{*}^{2}}

(S.5)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{R_{4}^{2}(\log d)^{3/2}\log m}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\frac{R_{p}^{2}(md)^{2/p}(\log d)^{2}\log m}{m\sigma_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\left[R_{p}d(\log d)^{(3/2)p-4}\log m\log(dm)\right]}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}^{p/(p-2)}}^{1/(p-2)},

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(d^{1/p}\sqrt{\tfrac{mb}{T}}\mathscr{A}_{b,p}L_{p}\right)^{\tfrac{p}{p+1}}\sqrt{1\lor\log d}+m\alpha_{b}

\displaystyle=:(I)+(II)+(III)+(IV)+(V)+(VI).

We now must choose sequences
a
and
b
to balance all the terms appearing on the right-hand side. Let
a=1\lor\lfloor T^{\gamma_{a}}\rfloor
and
b=1\lor\lfloor T^{\gamma_{b}}\rfloor
for
0\leq\gamma_{b}\leq\gamma_{a}<1
. Then
m\asymp T^{1-\gamma_{a}}
,
ma/T\asymp 1
,
mb/T\asymp T^{\gamma_{b}-\gamma_{a}}
. Comparing
(I)
,
(II)
and
(IV)
, it seems we cannot do much better than setting
\gamma_{a}=\gamma_{a}^{*}:=1/2
.
Since
\alpha_{n}\leq Kn^{-r}
then,
m\alpha_{b}\lesssim KT^{1-\gamma_{a}^{*}-r\gamma_{b}}=KT^{1/2-r\gamma_{b}}
. To equate
(V)
with
(VI)
(up to
\log
terms) and respect the fact that
0\leq\gamma_{b}\leq\gamma_{a}^{*}
we set

\gamma_{b}=\gamma_{b}^{*}:=\left(\frac{1/2+D_{p}/4}{r+D_{p}/2}\right)\land 1/2;\qquad D_{p}:=\frac{p}{p+1}.

Then,

(V)+(VI)\lesssim\frac{1}{T^{\phi(p,r)}}\left[d^{1/(p+1)}(\mathscr{A}_{b,p}L_{p})^{D_{p}}\sqrt{1\lor\log d}+K\right],

where

\phi(r,p):=\gamma_{a}^{*}+r\gamma_{b}^{*}-1=\begin{cases}\tfrac{D_{p}(r-1)}{4(r+D_{p}/2)}&;r\geq 1\\
\frac{r-1}{2}&;0\leq r<1.\end{cases}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{X},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L_{p}^{2}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}}\left[\frac{\mathscr{A}_{2}}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{\mathscr{A}_{1}(T^{\gamma_{b}^{*}})}{T^{(1/2)-\gamma_{b}^{*}}}+\frac{K_{d}^{1-2/p}}{T^{(r/2)(1-2/p)-1}}\right]\log T\log d

(S.6)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(L_{4}\mathscr{A}_{\sqrt{T},4})^{2}(\log d)^{3/2}\log T}{T^{1/4}\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\frac{(L_{p}\mathscr{A}_{\sqrt{T},p})^{2}d^{2/p}(\log d)^{2}\log T}{T^{1/2-1/p}\sigma_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\left[L_{p}\mathscr{A}_{\sqrt{T},p}d(\log d)^{(3/2)p-4}\log T\log(dT)\right]}{T^{1/4}\sigma_{*}^{p/(p-2)}}^{1/(p-2)},

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{T^{\phi(p,r)}}\left[d^{1/(p+1)}(\mathscr{A}_{T^{\gamma_{b}^{*}},p}L_{p})^{D_{p}}\sqrt{1\lor\log d}+K\right],

which concludes the proof of part (a).

The proof for part (b) runs parallel to the proof of part (a). In step 1, we replace Lemma
S.2
by Lemma
S.3
to conclude that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|A_{i,j}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}\lesssim\sqrt{a}
and thus
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|W_{i,j}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}\lesssim 1
, which in turn allow us to obtain

\displaystyle M_{\Sigma}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{m}{T}+\frac{mb}{T}+T\exp\left[-K_{1}(1-2/p)a^{\gamma_{1}}\right];\qquad p\in[2,\infty)

\displaystyle\mu_{4}

\displaystyle\leq m\max_{i\in[d]}\max_{j\in[m]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|W_{j,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{4}^{4}\lesssim m.

We apply Lemma
S.19
(b) followed (d) to write

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\max_{j,i}|W_{j,i}|\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}\lesssim\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(md)\max_{j,i}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|W_{j,i}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}\lesssim\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(md).

Similarly, we obtain
\mathbb{E}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\|_{\infty}^{p}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\max_{i\in[d]}|W_{j,i}|\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}^{p}\lesssim{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\max_{i\in[d]}|W_{j,i}|\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{p}\lesssim\big[\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(d)\big]^{p}
. Set
x=K_{x}\sqrt{\log d}
for some
K_{x}>0
large enough such that, by Lemma
S.5
, we have for
d\geq 2
and
a\geq 4\lor K_{x}(\log d)^{2/\gamma-1}
,

\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\|_{\infty}>x)=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{A}_{j}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\right\|_{\infty}>K_{x}\sqrt{\frac{T}{ma}}\sqrt{a\log d}\right)\leq(da)^{1-K_{x}^{\gamma}}+2d^{1-K^{2}_{x}}.

Then, by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,

H(x)\leq\max_{j\in[m]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\|_{\infty}^{8}\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\|_{\infty}>x)}\lesssim\left[\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(d)\right]^{4}\left[(da)^{1-K_{x}^{\gamma}}+2d^{1-K_{x}^{2}}\right]^{1/2}

Plug these bounds back into (
S.3
), we are left with

\displaystyle\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{\widetilde{A}},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{\frac{m}{T}+\frac{mb}{T}+T\exp\left[-K_{1}(1-2/p)a^{\gamma_{1}}\right]\right\}\frac{\log m\log d}{\sigma_{*}^{2}}

(S.7)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(\log d)^{3/2}\log m}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\frac{\big[\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(md)\big]^{2}(\log d)^{2}\log m}{m\sigma_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\log d\sqrt{\log m\log(dm)}\big[\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(d)\big]^{2}\big[(da)^{1-K^{\gamma}}+2d^{1-K^{2}}\big]^{1/4}}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(\log d)^{2}}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}}.

For step 2, we can then bound the third term in (
S.2
) by Lemma
S.5
. Set
s=K_{s}\sqrt{\frac{mb}{T}\log d\log T}
for some
K_{s}>0
large enough so that, for
d\geq 2
, and
mb\geq 4\lor K_{s}(\log d)^{2/\gamma-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{B}\|_{\infty}>s\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{j}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\right\|_{\infty}>K_{s}\sqrt{mb\log T\log d}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(dmb)^{1-K_{s}^{\gamma}(\log T)^{\gamma/2}}+2d^{1-K_{s}^{2}\log T}.

By plugging the bounds above back into (
S.2
) we obtain

\displaystyle\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{X},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{\frac{m}{T}+\frac{mb}{T}+T\exp\left[-K_{1}(1-2/p)a^{\gamma_{1}}\right]\right\}\frac{\log m\log d}{\sigma_{*}^{2}}

(S.8)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(\log d)^{3/2}\log m}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\frac{\big[\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(md)\big]^{2}(\log d)^{2}\log m}{m\sigma_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\log d\sqrt{\log m\log(dm)}\big[\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(d)\big]^{2}\big[(da)^{1-K^{\gamma}_{x}}+2d^{1-K_{x}^{2}}\big]^{1/4}}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(\log d)^{2}}{\sqrt{m}\sigma_{*}}+(dmb)^{1-K_{s}^{\gamma}(\log T)^{\gamma/2}}+2d^{1-K_{s}^{2}\log T}.

\displaystyle\qquad+\log d\sqrt{\tfrac{mb}{T}\log T}+m\exp(-K_{1}b^{\gamma_{1}}).

For the same reason discussed in the polynomial case, we cannot do much better than setting
a\asymp T^{1/2}
. Then,
m\asymp T^{1/2}
. Set
b\asymp(\log T)^{\gamma_{1}}
so that the last term is at most
T^{-1/4}
. Note that the numerator of the fourth term above can be bounded uniformly in
d
and
T
by a constant only depending on
\gamma
and the choice of
K_{x}>1
. Also,
\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{K^{2}_{s}\log T-1}\lesssim T^{-1}
provided that
K_{s}>1
as
d\geq 2
, and
\left[\frac{1}{d\sqrt{T}(\log T)^{\gamma_{1}}}\right]^{K^{\gamma}(\log T)^{\gamma/2}-1}\lesssim T^{-1/2}
by taking
K_{s}\geq 2^{1/\gamma}
. Finally,
\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(x)\lesssim(\log x)^{1/\gamma}
, for
x\geq\exp(1/\gamma)-1
by Lemma
S.19
(a). Result (b) follows.

\displaystyle\rho\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{X},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\log T)^{\gamma_{1}+1}\log d}{\sqrt{T}\sigma_{*}^{2}}

(S.9)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(\log d)^{3/2}\log T}{T^{1/4}\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\frac{\big[\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(d\sqrt{T})\big]^{2}(\log d)^{2}\log T}{\sqrt{T}\sigma_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\log d\sqrt{\log T\log(d\sqrt{T})}\big[\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(d)\big]^{2}\big[(d\sqrt{T})^{1-K^{\gamma}}+2d^{1-K^{2}}\big]^{1/4}}{T^{1/4}\sigma_{*}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(\log d)^{2}}{T^{1/4}\sigma_{*}}+\left(\frac{1}{d\sqrt{T}(\log T)^{\gamma_{1}}}\right)^{K^{\gamma}(\log T)^{\gamma/2}-1}+\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{K^{2}\log T-1}.

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\log d(\log T)^{\gamma_{1}+1}}{T^{1/4}}\log\left(1\lor d\right)+\frac{1}{T^{1/4}}.

Note that the numerator of the fourth term above can be bounded uniformly in
d
and
T
by a constant only depending on
\gamma
and the choice of
K>1
. Also,
\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{K^{2}\log T-1}\lesssim T^{-1}
as
d\geq 2
and
K>1
, and
\left[\frac{1}{d\sqrt{T}(\log T)^{\gamma_{1}}}\right]^{K^{\gamma}(\log T)^{\gamma/2}-1}\lesssim T^{-1/2}
by taking
K\geq 2^{1/\gamma}
. Finally,
\psi_{e^{\gamma}}^{-1}(x)\lesssim(\log x)^{1/\gamma}
for
x\geq\exp(1/\gamma)-1
by Lemma
S.19
(a). Result (b) follows.
∎

S.7
Auxiliary Lemmas

S.7.1
Concentration Inequalities for strong mixing sequences

Lemma S.1
.

Let
\{\boldsymbol{X}_{t}:t\in\mathbb{Z}\}
be a sequence of
d
-dimensional random vector with strong mixing coefficient
\{\alpha^{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
. For a non-negative integer
k
, define
\{\boldsymbol{Y}_{t}:=f(\boldsymbol{X}_{t},\dots,\boldsymbol{X}_{t-k}):t\in\mathbb{Z}\}
for some measurable
f:\mathbb{R}^{d(k+1)}\to\mathbb{R}^{q}
and denote its strong mixing coefficient by
\{\alpha^{\boldsymbol{Y}}_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
. Then
\alpha^{\boldsymbol{Y}}_{n}\leq\alpha^{\boldsymbol{X}}_{(n-k)\lor 0}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{s}^{t}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{s}^{t}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
(X_{s},\dots,X_{t})
and
(Y_{s},\dots,Y_{t})
, respectively where
-\infty\leq s\leq t\leq\infty
. Since
\mathcal{Y}_{-\infty}^{t}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{-\infty}^{t}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{t+n}^{\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{t+n-k}^{\infty}
, we have
\alpha^{\boldsymbol{Y}}_{n}\leq\alpha^{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n-k}
for
n\geq k
and
\alpha^{\boldsymbol{Y}}_{n}\leq\alpha^{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0}
for
0\leq n<k
.
∎

Lemma S.2
.

[based on Corollary 1.1 and Theorem 6.3 in
Rio 2017
] Let
S_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}
where
\{X_{t}:t\in[T]\}
is a sequence of zero mean real-valued random variables such that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}<\infty
for
t\in[T]
, and the mixing coefficients given by
\{\alpha_{m}:0\leq m<T\}
. Then, for
p\in[2,\infty)
and
T\in\mathbb{N}
,

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|S_{T}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\leq a_{p}\sqrt{T}L_{2,\alpha}+b_{p}T^{1/p}L_{p,\alpha},

where
L_{s,\alpha}:=\left\{\int_{0}^{1}[\alpha^{-1}(u)\land T]^{s-1}Q^{s}(u){du}\right\}^{1/s}
for
s>0
,
a_{p}
and
b_{p}
are positive constants only depending on
p
,
\alpha^{-1}(u):=\sum_{0\leq m<T}\mathds{1}\{u\leq\alpha_{m}\}
,
Q:=\max_{t\in[T]}Q_{t}
and
Q_{t}(u):=\sup\{x\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{P}(|X_{t}|>x)<u\}
.

If further,
X_{t}\in\mathcal{L}^{q}
for
t\in[T]
for some
q\in(p,\infty]
, then

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|S_{T}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\leq c_{p,q}\mathscr{A}_{p,q}\max_{t\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{q}\sqrt{T},

where
\mathscr{A}_{p,q}:=\left[\sum_{0\leq m<T}(m+1)^{p-2}\alpha_{m}^{1-p/q}\right]^{\tfrac{1}{p}}
and
c_{p,q}
is a positive constant only depending on
p
and
q
.

If further,
\alpha_{m}\leq(m+1)^{-r}
for some constant
r\geq 0
and all
m\in\mathbb{N}
then

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|S_{T}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\leq d_{p,q,r}\mathscr{R}_{p,q,r}(T)\max_{t\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{q};\qquad\mathscr{R}_{p,q,r}(T):=\begin{cases}\sqrt{T}&;r>\nu\\
(\log T+\lambda)^{\tfrac{1}{p}}\sqrt{T}&;r=\nu\\
T^{[\frac{1}{2}+\frac{p-1-r(1-p/q)}{p}]\land 1}&;r<\nu,\end{cases}

where
\nu:=\frac{(p-1)}{1-p/q}
and
d_{p,q,r}
is a positive constant only depending on
p
,
q
and
r
and
\lambda\approx 1.1
.

The first result, for
p=2
, follows from Corollary 1.1 in
Rio 2017
by setting
a_{2}=b_{2}=1
and bounding

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}[\alpha^{-1}(u)\land T]Q^{2}_{t}(u){du}\leq T\int_{0}^{1}[\alpha^{-1}(u)\land T]\left(\max_{t\in[T]}Q_{t}\right)^{2}(u){du}.

For
p>2
we rely on Theorem 6.3 in
Rio 2017
, the concavity of
x\mapsto x^{1/p}
and the result for
p=2
to write
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|S_{T}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\leq a_{p}\sqrt{T}L_{2,\alpha}+b_{p}T^{1/p}L_{p,\alpha}
with
a_{p}=2^{1+2(p+1)/p}p^{1/p}\sqrt{p+1}
and
b_{p}=\left[\frac{p}{p-1}4^{p+1}(p+1)^{p-1}\right]^{1/p}
.

For the second result, Markov’s inequality give us
\mathbb{P}(|X_{t}|\geq x)\leq\left({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{q}/x\right)^{q}
for
x>0
. Then,
Q_{t}(u)\leq\frac{{\left|\kern-0.75346pt\left|\kern-0.75346pt\left|X_{t}\right|\kern-0.75346pt\right|\kern-0.75346pt\right|}_{q}}{u^{1/p}}
and
Q(u):=\max_{t\in[t]}Q(u)\leq\frac{\mu_{q}}{u^{1/p}}
, where
\mu_{q}:=\max_{t\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{q}
. Then, for
s\in[2,p]
, we have

\displaystyle L_{s,q}\leq\mu_{q}\left\{\int_{0}^{1}[\alpha^{-1}(u)\land T]^{s-1}u^{-s/q}\mathsf{d}u\right\}^{1/s}\leq\mu_{q}\left\{\int_{0}^{1}[\alpha^{-1}(u)\land T]^{p-1}u^{-p/q}\mathsf{d}u\right\}^{1/s}.

Combine equation (C.10) in in
Rio 2017
, the bound
Q(u)\leq\frac{\mu_{q}}{u^{1/p}}
and the last expression to obtain

\displaystyle L_{s,q}\leq\mu_{q}\left(\frac{p-1}{1-p/q}\right)^{1/s}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{T-1}(m+1)^{p-2}\alpha_{m}^{1-s/q}\right]^{1/s},\qquad s\in[2,p].

We apply this bound to the first result, such that the second result follows with
c_{p,q}=2(a_{p}\lor b_{p})\left(\frac{p-1}{1-p/q}\right)^{1/s}
.

For the last result, if
\alpha_{m}\leq(m+1)^{-r}
then
\mathscr{A}_{p,q}^{p}\leq\sum_{m=1}^{T}m^{p-2-r(1-p/q)}
. For
r>\nu:=\frac{p-1}{1-p/q}
, the sum is convergent as
T\to\infty
, let
C_{1}:=\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathscr{A}_{p,q}(T)
. For
r=\nu
we have the harmonic series which is known to diverge at rate slower than
\log T+\lambda
where
\lambda\approx 1.1
. Finally, for
r<\nu
we have
T^{-1}\sum_{m=1}^{T}(m/T)^{p-2-r(1-p/q)}\leq\int_{0}^{1}x^{p-2-r(1-p/q)}\mathsf{d}x=\frac{1}{p-1-r(1-p/q)}=:C_{2}^{p}
. Thus,

\mathscr{A}_{p,q}\leq\begin{cases}C_{1}&;r>\nu\\
(\log T+\lambda)^{1/p}&;r=\nu;\\
C_{2}T^{\frac{p-1-r(1-p/q)}{p}}&;r<\nu.\end{cases}

Apply this last bound to the second result yields the last result with
d_{p,q,r}=c_{p,q}(1\lor C_{1}\lor C_{2})
.
∎

Lemma S.3
.

[based on Theorem 1 in
Merlevède, Peligrad and
Rio 2011
] Let
S_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}
where
\{X_{t}:t\in[T]\}
is a sequence of zero mean real-valued random variables such that

1.

There exist two positive constants
\gamma_{1}
and
K_{1}

such that the strong mixing coefficients of the sequence satisfy
\alpha(m)\leq\exp(-K_{1}m^{\gamma_{1}})
for any
1\leq m<T
and
T\geq 2
,

2.

There exist two positive constants
\gamma_{2}
and
K_{2}

such that
\sup_{1\leq t\leq T,T\in\mathbb{N}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma_{2}}}\leq K_{2}
,

3.

\gamma<1
where
\gamma
is defined by
1/\gamma=1/\gamma_{1}+1/\gamma_{2}
.

Then, there exist positive constants
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
,
C_{3}
and
C_{4}
depending only on
K_{2},K_{1},\gamma
and
\gamma_{1}
such that, for
x>0
and
T\geq 4
,

\mathbb{P}(|S_{T}|\geq x)\leq T\exp\left(-\frac{x^{\gamma}}{C_{1}}\right)+\exp\left[-\frac{x^{2}}{C_{2}(1+TV)}\right]+\exp\left\{-\frac{x^{2}}{C_{3}T}\exp\left[\frac{x^{\gamma(1-\gamma)}}{C_{4}(\log x)^{\gamma}}\right]\right\},

where
V
is a finite constant.

In particular, there a constant
C_{5}
only depending on
C_{3},C_{4}
and
\gamma
such that, for
x>1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|S_{T}|\geq x)\leq T\exp\left(-\frac{x^{\gamma}}{C_{1}}\right)+\exp\left[-\frac{x^{2}}{C_{2}(1+TV)}\right]+\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{C_{5}T}\right).

Furthermore, for some constant
C_{6}
only depending on
C_{i}
for
i\in[4]
,
\gamma_{1}
,
\gamma_{2}
and
V
,

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|S_{T}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\psi_{\gamma}}\leq C_{6}\sqrt{T},\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\psi_{\gamma}(x):=K_{\gamma}x\mathds{1}_{0\leq x<a_{\gamma}}+(\exp x^{\gamma}-1)\mathds{1}_{x\geq a_{\gamma}}

where
a_{\gamma}:=(1/\gamma)^{1/\gamma}
and
K_{\gamma}:=(\exp a_{\gamma}^{\gamma}-1)/a_{\gamma}
.

We verify the conditions (2.6), (2.7), and (2.8) of Theorem 1 in
Merlevède, Peligrad and
Rio 2011
, henceforth MPR. Assumption (b) ensures that
\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(|X_{i}/K_{2}|^{\gamma_{2}})\right]\leq 2
then following Remark 2 in MPR. Condition (2.7) is satisfied with
b=K_{2}
. From expression (2.5) in MPR we have
\tau(m)\leq 2\int_{0}^{2\alpha(m)}Q(u)du
, where
Q:=\sup_{1\leq t\leq T,T\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{|X_{t}|}
with
Q_{|X_{t}|}
denoting the quantile function of
|X_{t}|
. Cauchy-Schwartz inequality gives us

\tau(m)\leq 2\sqrt{2\alpha(m)\int_{0}^{1}Q^{2}(u)du}
. Given Assumption (b), the integral is finite. Let denote it by
M^{2}
. By Assumption (a) we have that
\tau(m)\leq 2\sqrt{2}M\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}K_{1}m^{\gamma_{1}}\right)
, then condition (2.6) is satisfied with
a=2\sqrt{2}M
and
c=\frac{1}{2}K_{1}
. The first result follows.

For the second result, note that the function
x\mapsto x^{(1-\gamma)}/\log x
is continuously differentiable and coercive for
x>1
, hence it attains its minimum over
x\in(1,\infty)
which is given by
C_{\gamma}:=e(1-\gamma)>0
. Define
C_{5}:=C_{3}/\exp\left(\frac{C_{\gamma}^{\gamma}}{C_{4}}\right)
, then for
x>1
the last term of the first result can be upper bounded by
\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{C_{5}T}\right)
, and the second result follows.

For the last result we have, by Fubini’s Theorem and
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\psi_{\gamma}\left(\frac{S_{T}}{\sqrt{T}C}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[|S_{T}|>C\psi^{-1}(x)\sqrt{T}\right]dx

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{a_{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}\left[|S_{T}|>\frac{C}{K_{\gamma}}x\sqrt{T}\right]dx+\int_{a_{\gamma}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{|S_{T}|>C\left[\log(1+x)\right]^{1/\gamma}\sqrt{T}\right\}dx

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

Lemma
S.2
give us
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|T^{-1/2}S_{T}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\leq K_{p}
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
. Then, for the first integral we have

I_{1}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[|T^{-1/2}S_{T}|>\frac{C}{K_{\gamma}}x\right]dx=\frac{K_{\gamma}}{C}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|T^{-1/2}S_{T}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{1}\leq\frac{K_{\gamma}}{C}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|T^{-1/2}S_{T}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq\frac{K_{\gamma}K_{2}}{C}.

For the second one, note that
a_{\gamma}>1
, then

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{a_{\gamma}}^{\infty}T\left(1+x\right)^{-\tfrac{C^{\gamma}T^{\gamma/2}}{C_{1}}}dx+\int_{a_{\gamma}}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}[\log(1+x)]^{2/\gamma}}{C_{2}(T^{-1}+V)}\right)+\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{C_{5}T}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{a_{\gamma}}^{\infty}T\left(1+x\right)^{-\tfrac{C^{\gamma}T^{\gamma/2}}{C_{1}}}dx+\int_{a_{\gamma}}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}[\log(1+x)]^{2/\gamma}}{C_{7}}\right)dx:=I_{3}+I_{4},

where
C_{7}:=C_{2}(1+V)\lor C_{5}
. For
C^{\gamma}/C_{1}>1
, we can bound
I_{3}
as

I_{3}=\frac{T(1+a_{\gamma})^{1-\tfrac{C^{\gamma}T^{\gamma/2}}{C_{1}}}}{\tfrac{C^{\gamma}T^{\gamma/2}}{C_{1}}-1}\leq\frac{T^{1-\gamma/2}(1+a_{\gamma})^{1-T^{\gamma/2}}}{\tfrac{C^{\gamma}}{C_{1}}-1}\leq\frac{M_{\gamma}}{\tfrac{C^{\gamma}}{C_{1}}-1},

where
M_{\gamma}:=\sup_{T\geq 4}\big[T^{1-\gamma/2}(1+a_{\gamma})^{1-T^{\gamma/2}}\big]
which is finite because
a_{\gamma}>0
. For the
C^{2}/C_{7}>1
,
\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}[\log(1+x)]^{2/\gamma}}{C_{7}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-[\log(1+x)]^{2/\gamma}\right)
and

\displaystyle\int_{a_{\gamma}}^{\infty}\exp\left(-[\log(1+x)]^{2/\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq((a_{\gamma}\lor 2)-a_{\gamma})+\int_{a_{\gamma}\lor 2}^{\infty}\exp\left(-[\log(1+x)]^{2/\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq 2+\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\left(-[\log(1+x)]^{2}\right)dx\leq 4,

where we use
\log 3>1
and the last integral equals
e^{1/4}\sqrt{\pi}(\mathsf{erf}(1/2)+1)/2\leq 2
with
\mathsf{erf}
is Gauss error function. Then, by the dominated convergence theorem, we conclude that
\lim_{C\to\infty}I_{4}=0
, hence there exist
C_{8}>0
such
I_{4}\leq 1/3
for
C\geq C_{8}
.

Therefore,
\mathbb{E}\psi_{\gamma}(\tfrac{S_{T}}{\sqrt{T}C})\leq I_{1}+I_{3}+I_{4}\leq 1
for
C\geq C_{6}:=3K_{\gamma}K_{2}\lor(2C_{1})^{1/\gamma}\lor(2C_{7})^{1/2}\lor\big[C_{1}(1+3M)\big]^{1/\gamma}\lor C_{8}
.
∎

Lemma S.4
.

Let
\boldsymbol{S}_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}
where
\{\boldsymbol{X}_{t}:=(X_{1,t},\dots,X_{n,t})^{\prime}:t\in[T]\}
is a sequence of zero mean
n
-dimensional random vector such that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p+\epsilon}<\infty
for
i\in[n]
and
t\in[T]
for
p\in[2,\infty)
and
\epsilon>0
. Then, for
T\in\mathbb{N}
and
x>0
,

\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{T}\|_{\infty}\geq x)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{c_{p,\epsilon}\sqrt{T}\mathscr{A}_{i}(T)\max_{t\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p+\epsilon}}{x}\right)^{p},

where
\mathscr{A}_{i}(T):=\left[\sum_{0\leq m<T}(m+1)^{p-2}\alpha_{i,m}^{1-p/(p+\epsilon)}\right]^{\tfrac{1}{p}}
,
\alpha_{i,m}
is the strong mixing coefficient of
\{X_{i,t}\}_{t}
for
1\leq i\leq n
, and
c_{p},\epsilon
is a constant depending only on
p
and
\epsilon
.

The result follows from the union bound, Markov inequality, and Lemma
S.2
.
∎

Lemma S.5
.

Let
\boldsymbol{S}_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}
where
\{\boldsymbol{X}_{t}:=(X_{1,t},\dots,X_{n,t})^{\prime}:1\leq t\leq T\}
be a sequence of zero mean
n
-dimensional random variables and write
\boldsymbol{S}_{T}
for its sum. Assume:

1.

There exist two positive constants
\gamma_{1}
and
K_{1}

such that the strong mixing coefficients
\alpha_{i,m}\leq\exp(-K_{1}m^{\gamma_{1}})
for
1\leq m<T
,
1\leq i\leq n
, and
T\geq 2
;

2.

There exist two positive constants
\gamma_{2}
and
K_{2}

such that
\sup_{1\leq t\leq T,T\in\mathbb{N}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{i,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma_{2}}}\leq K_{2}
,

3.

\gamma<1
where
\gamma
is defined by
1/\gamma=1/\gamma_{1}+1/\gamma_{2}
.

Then there exist positive constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
depending only on
K_{2},K_{1},\gamma,\gamma_{1}
, and the covariance structure such that, for
n\geq 2
,
T\geq 4\lor C_{1}(\log n)^{(2/\gamma)-1}
, and
K\geq 1/\sqrt{C_{1}C_{2}(\log 2)^{2/\gamma}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{T}\|_{\infty}\geq K\sqrt{C_{2}T\log n})\leq(nT)^{1-K^{\gamma}}+2n^{1-K^{2}}.

In particular,
\|\boldsymbol{S}_{T}\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\big(\sqrt{T\log n}\big)
whenever
\frac{(\log n)^{(2/\gamma)-1}}{T}=o(1)
.

Write
\boldsymbol{S}_{T}=(S_{1,T},\dots,S_{n,T})^{\prime}
where
S_{i,T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{i,t}
for
i\in[n]
. The union bound followed by the second result in Lemma
S.3
yields, for every
x>1
and
T\geq 4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{T}\|_{\infty}\geq x)

\displaystyle\leq n\max_{i}\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{i,T}\|_{\infty}\geq x)

\displaystyle\leq n\left[T\exp\left(-\frac{x^{\gamma}}{C_{1}}\right)+\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{C_{2}(1+TV)}\right)+\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{C_{5}T}\right)\right].

Set
x=K\left[(C_{1}\log(nT))^{1/\gamma}\lor\sqrt{C_{6}T\log(n)}\right]
for some
K>0
where
C_{6}:=(C_{2}+V)\lor C_{5}
. For large enough
T
, we have that
x>1
and, therefore,
\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{S}_{T}\|_{\infty}\geq x)\leq(nT)^{1-K^{\gamma}}+2n^{1-K^{2}}
. Notice that that the first in brackets is no larger than the second one provided that
(C_{1}\log(nT))^{2/\gamma}\leq C_{6}T\log(n)
, which in turn is implied by
T\geq C_{7}(\log n)^{(2/\gamma)-1}
where
C_{7}=C_{6}^{-1}(2C_{1})^{2/\gamma}
. Therefore, for
T\geq C_{7}(\log n)^{(2/\gamma)-1}
we have that
x=K\sqrt{C_{6}T\log n}
and
x>1
whenever
K\geq 1/\sqrt{C_{6}C_{7}(\log 2)^{2/\gamma}}
.
∎

S.7.2
Factor Model Estimation

Lemma S.6
.

Let
a_{j}
and
b_{j}
denote the
j
-th eigenvalue in decreasing order of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
and
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\prime}
respectively. Then, under Assumption
3
(b) and
(c)
: (a)
b_{j}\asymp n
for
1\leq j\leq r
; (b)
\max_{j\leq n}|a_{j}-b_{j}\lesssim 1
; and (c)
a_{j}\asymp n
for
1\leq j\leq r
.

Result
(a)
follows from the fact that the
r
eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}
are also (the only
r
non-zero) eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\prime}
and Assumption
3
(b). Part
(b)
follows from Weyl’s inequality that implies
\max_{j\leq n}|a_{j}-b_{j}|\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\prime}\|\lesssim 1
, where the last equality follows from Assumption
3
(c). Finally, result
(c)
follows from part
(a)
and
(b)
and the (reverse) triangle inequality.
∎

Recall that
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
is the
(n\times n)
covariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{U}_{t}=\boldsymbol{Z}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{W}_{t}
. Let
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}:=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}^{\prime}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
the same as
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
but with
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}
replaced by the estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}
. Let
\widehat{a}_{j}
denote the
j
-th eigenvalue in decreasing order of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
.

Lemma S.7
.

Under the Assumptions
3
and
2
, let
\varrho_{1}
be a non-negative sequence of
n
and
T
such that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{1}
, then:

1.

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{1}+g_{\alpha}(n)/\sqrt{T}

2.

\max_{j\leq n}|\widehat{a}_{j}-a_{j}|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}n(\varrho_{1}+g_{\alpha}(n)/\sqrt{T})

3.

\widehat{a}_{j}\asymp_{P}n
for
j\leq r
provided that
\varrho_{1}+g_{\alpha}(n)/\sqrt{T}\lesssim 1
,

where
g_{\alpha}(n)=\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}n^{4/p}
under Assumptions (
2
.c) and
g_{\alpha}(n)=\sqrt{\log n}
under Assumptions (
2
.d).

Part (a) follows by the triangle and Lemma
S.4
or Lemma
S.5
, since
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{\max}\leq\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\|_{\max}+\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{1}+g(n)/\sqrt{T}
. Part (b) follows from Weyl’s inequality, the fact that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|\leq n\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{\max}
and part
(a)
. Part
(c)
follows from the triangle inequality combined with part
(b)
and Lemma
S.6
(c).
∎

The Lemmas
S.8
-
S.11
below are an adaption of Lemmas 8–10 in
Fan, Liao and Mincheva 2013
, henceforth FLM, to include the estimation error in the sample covariance matrix. To avoid confusion and make it easier for the reader to follow through with the changes we use the same notation adopted in FLM. In particular, if
\delta_{i,t}
denotes the
(i,t)
element of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}:=\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}-\boldsymbol{R}
then
\widetilde{U}_{i,t}=U_{i,t}+\delta_{i,t}
for
i\in[n]
and
t\in[T]
. We consider that
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}
for some non-negative sequence
\varrho_{R}
depending on
n
and
T
.

Define:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{U}}_{s}^{\prime}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{U}}_{t}}{n}-\frac{\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t})}{n}=\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}}{n}-\frac{\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t})}{n}\right)+\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}}{n}+\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}}{n}+\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}}{n}\right)=:\zeta_{st}+\zeta_{st}^{*}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}_{st}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\boldsymbol{F}_{s}^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{U}_{i,t}}{n}=\frac{\boldsymbol{F}_{s}^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}U_{i,t}}{n}+\frac{\boldsymbol{F}_{s}^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\delta_{i,t}}{n}=:\eta_{st}+\eta_{st}^{*}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\xi}_{st}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\boldsymbol{F}_{t}^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{U}_{is}}{n}=\frac{\boldsymbol{F}_{t}^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}U_{is}}{n}+\frac{\boldsymbol{F}_{t}^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\delta_{is}}{n}=\xi_{st}+\xi_{st}^{*}.

Lemma S.8
.

Under Assumption
2
:

1.

\zeta_{st}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/\sqrt{n}

2.

\eta_{st}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/\sqrt{n}

3.

\xi_{st}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/\sqrt{n}

4.

\zeta_{st}^{*}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}+\varrho_{R}^{2}

5.

\max_{s,t\leq T}\zeta_{st}^{*}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}g(nT)\varrho_{R}+\varrho_{R}^{2}

6.

\eta_{st}^{*}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}

7.

\xi_{st}^{*}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}
,

where
g(x)=x^{1/p}
under Assumptions (
2
.c) and
g(x)=[\log x]^{1/\gamma_{2}}
under Assumptions (
2
.d);

Parts
(a)
-
(c)
are straightforward. For
(d)
we have that
\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1
and
\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\max}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}^{2}
. Then, the other two terms in parentheses in the definition of
\zeta_{st}^{*}
are
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}
by the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality. Part
(e)
and
(f)
follows by similar arguments. Therefore,

\max_{t\leq T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}\right)^{2}=\max_{t\leq T}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{U}_{t}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\max}^{2}\left(\max_{t\leq T}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{t}\|_{1}/n\right)^{2}

and

\zeta_{st}^{*}\leq\|\boldsymbol{U}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}\|_{\infty}+\|\boldsymbol{U}_{t}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}\|_{\infty}+\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\max}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\max}^{2}.

∎

Lemma S.9
.

Under Assumption
2
:

1.

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{F}_{js}\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t})\right]^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/T

2.

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{F}_{js}\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R}+\varrho_{R}^{2}\right)^{2}

3.

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{F}_{js}\widetilde{\eta}_{st}\right]^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R}\right)^{2}

4.

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{F}_{js}\xi_{st}\right]^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left(1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R}\right)^{2}

5.

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{F}_{t}\right\|^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/T+(1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R}+\varrho_{R}^{2})^{2}
.

Part (a) is unaltered by the presence of a pre-estimation, so it follows directly from Lemma 8(a) in FLM. For part (b), we have that for
s,l\in[n]
and
j\in[r]
by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{F}_{js}\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}\right]^{2}\leq\left[\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{s,l=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}\widetilde{\zeta}_{lt}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

Since
\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}=\zeta_{st}+\zeta_{st}^{*}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R}+\varrho_{R}^{2})
by Lemma
S.8
, the term in parentheses is
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R}+\varrho_{R}^{2})^{2}
. Result
(b)
follows. For (c), by the triangle inequality and Lemma 8(c) in FLM, we have that
\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{ji}\widetilde{u}_{i,t}\|\leq\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{ji}U_{i,t}\|+\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{ji}\delta_{i,t}\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{n}+n\varrho_{R}
. Then, we conclude

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\widetilde{\eta}_{st}]^{2}\leq\frac{1}{Tn^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{U}_{i,t}\lambda_{i}\|^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/n+\varrho_{R}/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R}^{2}.

The proof of part (d) is analogous to (c) and is omitted.
For (e), let
[\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{F}_{t}]_{j}
denote the
j
-th entry of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{F}_{t}
. Since
\boldsymbol{V}/n
is bounded away from zero by Lemma
S.7
(c), the fact that
(a+b+c+d)^{2}\leq 4(a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}+d^{2})
and using (
S.3
), we have that
\max_{j\leq r}T^{-1}\sum_{t}[\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{F}_{t}]_{j}
is upper bounded by some constant
C<\infty
times

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{j\leq r}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{F}_{js}\frac{\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t})}{n}\right]^{2}+\max_{j\leq r}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{f}_{js}\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+\max_{j\leq r}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{F}_{js}\widetilde{\eta}_{st}\right)^{2}+\max_{j\leq r}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{f}_{js}\widetilde{\xi}_{st}\right)^{2}\right\}.

The result follows by applying the bounds from (a)–(d) to each of the terms above.

∎

Lemma S.10
.

Under Assumptions
2
and
3
:

1.

\max_{t\leq T}\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t})\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/\sqrt{T})

2.

\max_{t\leq T}\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[g(T)/\sqrt{n}+g(nT)\varrho_{R}+\varrho_{R}^{2}\right]

3.

\max_{t\leq T}\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\widetilde{\eta}_{st}\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[g(T)/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R}\right]

4.

\max_{t\leq T}\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\xi_{st}\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[g(T)(1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R})\right]
,

where
g(x)=x^{1/p}
under Assumption (
2
.c) and
g(x)=[\log x]^{1/\gamma_{2}}
under Assumption (
2
.d).

Part (a) is unaltered by pre-estimation, so it follows directly from Lemma 9(a) in FLM. For part (b), from the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\max_{t\leq T}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}\right\|\leq\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\|^{2}\max_{t\leq T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

The first summation inside the parentheses equals
r
due to the normalization. For the second summation, by the triangle inequality, we have
\max_{t\leq T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}^{2}\leq\max_{t\leq T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\zeta_{st}^{2}+2\max_{t\leq T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\zeta_{st}\zeta_{st}^{*}+\max_{t\leq T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}{\zeta_{st}^{*}}^{2}
. For the first term, the maximum inequality followed by Assumption
3
(e) yields

\max_{t\leq T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\zeta_{st}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\psi_{p/2}^{-1}(T)\max_{s,t}\|\zeta^{2}\|_{\psi_{p/2}}\right]\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\psi_{p/2}^{-1}(T)\max_{s,t}\|\zeta\|_{\psi}^{2}\right]\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{g_{1}(T)}{n}\right].

where
g_{1}(x)=x^{2/p}
or
g_{1}(x)=[\log x]^{2/\gamma_{2}}
.
The last one is
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}(g(nT)\varrho_{R}+\varrho_{R}^{2})^{2}
by Lemma
S.8
(d). Then, by Cauchy-Schwartz, we have that

\max_{t\leq T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{g_{1}(T)/n}+g(nT)\varrho_{R}+\varrho_{R}^{2})^{2}
and result (b) follows.

For (c), by the triangle inequality we have that
\max_{t\leq T}\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{U}_{i,t}\|\leq\max_{t\leq T}\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}U_{i,t}\|+\max_{t\leq T}\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\delta_{i,t}\|
. For the first term, the maximum inequality followed by Assumption
3
(f) yields

\max_{t\leq T}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}\right\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{g(T)}{\sqrt{n}}\max_{t}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}\right\|\right]\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[g(T)/\sqrt{n}\right].

The second term is upper bounded by
r\|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\|_{\max}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R})
by Assumption
3
(d). We obtain the result since

\max_{t\leq T}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\widetilde{\eta}_{st}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\boldsymbol{F}_{s}^{\prime}\right\|\max_{t\leq T}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{U}_{i,t}\right\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{g(T)}{\sqrt{n}}+\varrho_{R}\right].

(S.1)

For (d), by the triangle inequality,
\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{s}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{U}_{is}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\|\leq\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{s}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}U_{is}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\|+\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{s}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\delta_{is}\widehat{F}_{is}\|
. Lemma 9(d) of FLM shows that the first term is
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/\sqrt{n}
. For the second term, for each
j\in[r]
:

\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{s}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\delta_{is}\widehat{F}_{js}\right\|^{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\delta_{is}\right\|^{2}\widehat{F}_{js}\right)\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\widehat{F}_{js}^{2}\right)\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}^{2}).

Thus,
\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{s}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{U}_{i,t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R})
and by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality:

\max_{t\leq T}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\xi_{st}\right\|\leq\max_{t\leq T}\left\|\boldsymbol{F}_{t}\right\|\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{s}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{U}_{i,t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}\right\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[g(T)(1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R})\right].

(S.2)

∎

Lemma S.11
.

Let
\varrho_{1}+\psi^{-1}(n^{2})/\sqrt{T}\lesssim 1
where
\varrho_{1}
is defined in Lemma
S.7
. Then, under Assumption
2
we have:

1.

\|\boldsymbol{V}^{-1}\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/n

2.

\|\boldsymbol{H}\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1

3.

\|\boldsymbol{H}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{H}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\|_{F}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/\sqrt{T}+1/\sqrt{n}+\varrho_{R}

4.

\max_{i\leq n}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{R}_{i,t}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}(h(nT)+\varrho_{R})+g_{\alpha}(n)/\sqrt{T}+1

5.

\max_{i\leq n}\max_{j\leq r}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}F_{jt}\widetilde{U}_{i,t}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}g_{\alpha}(n)/\sqrt{T}+\varrho_{R}
,

where
g_{\alpha}(x)=\mathscr{A}_{\alpha}x^{2/p}
or
g(x)=\sqrt{\log x}
, and
h(x)=x^{1/p}
or
h(x)=[\log x]^{1/\gamma_{2}}
.

We have that
\boldsymbol{V}^{-1}=\mathsf{diag}\,(1/\widehat{a}_{1},\dots,1/\widehat{a}_{r})
and
1/\widehat{a}_{j}\asymp_{P}1/n
for
j\leq r
by Lemma
S.7
(c). The result (a) then follows. The normalization tell us
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}\|=\sqrt{T}
, Lemma 11(a) in FLM give us
\|\boldsymbol{F}\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{T})
,
\|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\|=\widetilde{a}_{1}\asymp n
by Lemma
S.6
(a), and from part (a) we have
\|\boldsymbol{V}^{-1}\|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/n)
. Result (b) then follows since by definition
\boldsymbol{H}:=T^{-1}\boldsymbol{V}^{-1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}
.
For (c), we have by the triangle inequality,

\|\boldsymbol{H}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{H}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\|_{F}\leq\|\boldsymbol{H}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{H}-\boldsymbol{H}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}/T\boldsymbol{H}\|_{F}+\|\boldsymbol{H}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}/T\boldsymbol{H}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\|_{F}

For the first term, we have

\|\boldsymbol{H}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{I}_{r}-\boldsymbol{F}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}/T)\boldsymbol{H}\|_{F}\leq\|\boldsymbol{H}\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{I}_{r}-\boldsymbol{F}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}/T\|_{F}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}1/\sqrt{T}.

The second term is equal to
\|\boldsymbol{H}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}/T\boldsymbol{H}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}^{\prime}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}/T\|_{F}
.

For (d) we have

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{R}_{i,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widehat{R}_{i,t}^{2}-R_{i,t}^{2})+\max_{i\leq n}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}R_{i,t}^{2}-\mathbb{E}(R_{i,t}^{2})+\max_{i\leq n}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}(R_{i,t}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,t}|\widehat{R}_{i,t}^{2}-R_{i,t}^{2}|+\max_{i\leq n}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}R_{i,t}^{2}-\mathbb{E}(R_{i,t}^{2})+\max_{i,t}\mathbb{E}(R_{i,t}^{2}).

The last term is
\lesssim 1
by Assumption
2
(c) or (d), the middle term
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}g(n)/\sqrt{T}
. The first term is no larger then
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\max}(2\|\boldsymbol{R}\|_{\max}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\max})\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}(h(nT)+\varrho_{R}))
. The result (d) then follows.

For (e) we have for each
j\leq r
:

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\max_{j\leq r}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t}F_{jt}\widetilde{U}_{i,t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,j}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t}F_{jt}U_{i,t}\right|+\max_{i,j}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t}F_{jt}\delta_{i,t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,j}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t}F_{jt}U_{i,t}\right|+\max_{i,j}\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t}F_{jt}^{2}T^{-1}\sum_{t}\delta_{i,t}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

The first term is
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}g(n)/\sqrt{T}
by the maximum inequality and Assumption
2
and the second is
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{R}
.
∎

S.7.3
Penalized Regression Results

Lemma S.12
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}:=\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}/T
and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}:=\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{V}/T
where
\boldsymbol{U}
and
\boldsymbol{V}
are
(T\times n)
matrices, then

\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\|_{\max}\leq\|\boldsymbol{U}-\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\max}(2\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\max}+\|\boldsymbol{U}-\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\max}).

Furthermore, if
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\|_{\max}\leq\alpha\kappa(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}},\mathcal{S},3)/(|\mathcal{S}|(1+\zeta)^{2})
for
\mathcal{S}\subseteq[n]
,
\zeta>0
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
, then for
\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\zeta\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}}\|_{1}
we have

(1-\alpha)\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\boldsymbol{x}\leq\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}\boldsymbol{x}\leq(1+\alpha)\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\boldsymbol{x}.

Take the infimum of the expression above over
\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\zeta\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}}\|_{1}\}
to conclude

(1-\alpha)\kappa^{2}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}},\mathcal{S},\zeta)\leq\kappa^{2}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}},\mathcal{S},\zeta)\leq(1+\alpha)\kappa^{2}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}},\mathcal{S},\zeta).

By the (reverse) triangle inequality we have
\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}-\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\max}\leq\|\boldsymbol{U}-\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\max}
. Also,
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\|_{\max}=\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}|T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}U_{i,t}U_{jt}-V_{i,t}V_{ijt}|\leq\max_{i,j,t}|U_{i,t}U_{jt}-V_{i,t}V_{jt}|
and
|U_{i,t}U_{jt}-V_{i,t}V_{jt}|\leq|(U_{i,t}-V_{i,t})U_{jt}+(U_{jt}-V_{jt})V_{i,t}|\leq\|\boldsymbol{U}-\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\max}(\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}+\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\max}).
. Combine the 3 bounds to obtain the first result.

For the second part of the lemma notice that for any
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we have
|\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\boldsymbol{x}|=|\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}})\boldsymbol{x}|\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\|_{\max}\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{1}^{2}
. Also, if
\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\zeta\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}}\|_{1}
we have that
\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{1}=\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}}\|_{1}+|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq(1+\zeta)\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}}\|_{1}\leq(1+\zeta)\sqrt{\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\boldsymbol{x}|\mathcal{S}|}/\kappa(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}},\mathcal{S},\zeta)
where the last inequality follows from the definition of compatibility condition. Thus
|\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\boldsymbol{x}|\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\|_{\max}(1+\zeta)^{2}\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\boldsymbol{x}|\mathcal{S}|/\kappa(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}},\mathcal{S},\zeta)\leq\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\boldsymbol{x}/2
, where the last inequality follows from the definition of compatibility condition. Therefore, we have that
(1-\alpha)\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\boldsymbol{x}\leq\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{U}}\boldsymbol{x}\leq(1+\alpha)\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{V}}\boldsymbol{x}
whenever
\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\zeta\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\mathcal{S}}\|_{1}
.
∎

Lemma S.13
.

Let
\boldsymbol{W}:=(\boldsymbol{U},\boldsymbol{V})
and
\boldsymbol{Z}:=(\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{Y})
be
T\times(n+1)
matrices such that
\|\boldsymbol{W}-\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{\max}\leq C_{1}
and
\|\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{\max}\leq C_{2}
, then for any
\boldsymbol{\delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we have

\|\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}-\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{\delta})/T-\boldsymbol{X}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\delta})/T\|_{\infty}\leq(1+\|\boldsymbol{\delta}\|_{1})C_{1}(2C_{2}+C_{1})

For convenience let
q:=V-U\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
and
r:=Y-X\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
, then Hölder’s inequality gives us
\|r\|_{\infty}\leq(1+\|\delta\|_{1})\|Z\|_{\max}\leq(1+\|\delta\|_{1})C_{2}
and
\|q-r\|_{\infty}\leq(1+\|\delta\|_{1})\|W-Z\|_{\max}\leq(1+\|\delta\|_{1})C_{1}
. From the (reverse) triangle inequality we obtain
\|q\|_{\infty}\leq\|q-r\|_{\infty}+\|r\|_{\infty}\leq(1+\|\delta\|_{1})(C_{1}+C_{2})
. Now, following the same steps in the proof of the previous Lemma, we can upper bound the right-hand side of the display by
\|U-X\|_{\max}\|q\|_{\infty}+\|q-r\|_{\infty}\|X\|_{\max}
, which in turn can be upper bounded by the left-hand side of the display.
∎

S.7.4
Inference Procedure Results

Lemma S.14
.

Let
X
and
Y
be random elements defined in the same probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
taking values in the metric space
(S,d)
. Then for measurable
A
and
\delta\geq 0

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}(Y\in A\setminus A^{-\delta})-\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(X\in A)-\mathbb{P}(Y\in A)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(Y\in A^{\delta}\setminus A)+\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta),

where
A^{\delta}:=\{x\in S:d(x,A)\leq\delta\}
,
A^{-\delta}:=S\setminus(S\setminus A)^{\delta}
and
d(x,A):=\inf_{y\in A}d(x,y)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a class of measurable subsets of
S
then

\rho_{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\leq\inf_{\delta>0}\big[\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta)+\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}(Y,\delta)\big],

where
\rho_{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y):=\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}|\mathbb{P}(X\in A)-\mathbb{P}(Y\in A)|
and
\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}(Y,\delta):=\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}(Y\in A^{\delta}\setminus A^{-\delta})
.

In particular, if
d
is induced by a norm
\|\cdot\|
we have for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta>0

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}(t-\delta\leq\|Y\|\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\|X-Y\|>\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|X\|\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\|Y\|\leq t)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(t<\|Y\|\leq t+\delta)+\mathbb{P}(\|X-Y\|>\delta).

For the right hand side, we use
\{X\in A\}\cap\{d(X,Y)\leq\delta\}\subseteq\{Y\in A^{\delta}\}
to write

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X\in A)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(X\in A,d(X,Y)\leq\delta)+\mathbb{P}(X\in A,d(X,Y)>\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(Y\in A^{\delta})+\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(Y\in A)+\mathbb{P}(Y\in A^{\delta}\setminus A)+\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta).

For the left hand side, we use
\{Y\in A^{-\delta}\}\cap\{d(X,Y)\leq\delta\}\subseteq\{X\in A\}
to write

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X\in A)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(Y\in A^{-\delta},d(X,Y)\leq\delta)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(Y\in A^{-\delta})-\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(Y\in A)-\mathbb{P}(Y\in A\setminus A^{-\delta})-\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta).

The first result follows. For the second one we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}(X\in A)-\mathbb{P}(Y\in A)|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta)+\mathbb{P}(Y\in A^{\delta}\setminus A)\lor\mathbb{P}(Y\in A\setminus A^{-\delta})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta)+\mathbb{P}(Y\in A^{\delta}\setminus A^{-\delta}).

Take the supremum over
A\in\mathcal{A}
we have
\rho_{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\leq\mathbb{P}(d(X,Y)>\delta)+\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}(Y,\delta)
. Take the infimum over
\delta>0

to obtain the second result.

For the last one, take
A=B_{t}
where
B_{t}
is
\|\cdot\|
-closed ball of radius
t
centered at the origin for
t\geq 0
and the empty set otherwise, then
\{X\in A\}=\{\|X\|\leq t\}
and
\{Y\in A\}=\{\|Y\|\leq t\}
. Also
\{Y\in A^{\delta}\setminus A\}=\{t<\|Y\|\leq t+\delta\}
since
A^{\delta}=B_{t+\delta}
and
\{Y\in A\setminus A^{-\delta}\}=\{t-\delta\leq\|Y\|\leq t\}
since
A^{-\delta}
is the open ball of radius
t-\delta

∎

Lemma S.15
.

Let
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{U}
for some positive sequence of
n
and
T
, then

\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime})\right\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{T}\varrho_{U}^{2}+\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}\left(\frac{n^{9/p}}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{n^{6/p}}{T^{1/2-1/p}}\right)+\frac{1}{n^{1/2-9/p}}.

By the triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime})\right\|_{\max}\leq\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U})(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U})^{\prime}\right\|_{\max}+2\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\boldsymbol{U}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U})^{\prime}\right\|_{\max}.

For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U})(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U})^{\prime}\right\|_{\max}\leq\sqrt{T}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\sqrt{T}\varrho_{U}^{2}).

For the second term we use decomposition (
S.5.2
) to write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}U_{i,t}(\widehat{U}_{jt}-U_{jt})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}U_{i,t}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{j}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{F}_{t}+\widehat{R}_{jt}-R_{jt})

\displaystyle=\left[(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{j})+\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{j}\right]^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}U_{i,t}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{F}_{t})

\displaystyle\quad+\left[(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{j})+(\boldsymbol{H}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{H}-I_{r})\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{j}\right]^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}U_{i,t}\boldsymbol{F}_{t}

\displaystyle\quad+(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j})^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}U_{i,t}\boldsymbol{X}_{jt}.

Apply Cauchy-Schwartz inequality in each term followed by the triangle inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\boldsymbol{U}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U})^{\prime}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\max_{j\leq n}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{j}\|+\sqrt{r}\|\boldsymbol{H}\|\|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\|_{\max}\right]\max_{i\leq n}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}U_{i,t}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{F}_{t})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left[\max_{j\leq n}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{j}\|+\sqrt{r}\|\boldsymbol{H}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{H}-I_{r}\|\|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\|_{\max}\right]\max_{i\leq n}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}U_{i,t}\boldsymbol{F}_{t}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{j\leq n}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}\|\max_{i,j\leq n}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}U_{i,t}\boldsymbol{X}_{jt}\right\|.

(S.3)

Recall
\delta_{i,t}:=\widehat{R}_{i,t}-R_{i,t}
. From (
S.1
) and Holder’s inequality, we conclude that

\max_{i,t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\delta_{i,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}\lesssim{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{-1}\|\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i}\|_{\infty}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\|\boldsymbol{X}_{i,t}\|_{\infty}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\lesssim\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}/\sqrt{T},

where
\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}
is defined in Theorem
1
. We now use this last result to show that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\|(\boldsymbol{V}/n)(\boldsymbol{F}_{t}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{F}_{t})\|_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}=O\left(\frac{\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

(S.4)

which in turns uses identity (
S.3
) and the fact that for any random variable
A_{st}
, by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and the normalization
\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}/T=\boldsymbol{I}_{r}
, we have
\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}A_{st}\|\leq\sqrt{r}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}A_{st}^{2}\right)^{1/2}
. Thus

g(A_{st}):={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{s}A_{st}\right\|\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}\leq\sqrt{r}\left[{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}A_{st}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}\right].

1.

Set
A_{st}=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t})/n
, then
g(A_{st})\lesssim 1/\sqrt{T}
.

2.

Set
A_{st}=\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{U}}_{s}^{\prime}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{U}}_{t}-\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}))/n
. By the triangle inequality,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}A_{st}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}\leq\max_{s\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}
, and
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{\zeta}_{st}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\zeta_{st}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\zeta_{st}^{*}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}
. The first term is
\lesssim 1/\sqrt{n}
by Assumption
2
(c.2). The second can be upper bounded by
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\boldsymbol{U}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}/n\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{t}/n\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{s}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}/n\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}\lesssim\max_{i\in[n]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|U_{i,s}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\delta_{i,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}+\max_{i\in[n]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\delta_{i,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}^{2}
. Thus
g(A_{st})\lesssim 1/\sqrt{n}+\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}/\sqrt{T}
.

3.

Set
A_{st}=\widetilde{\eta}_{st}:=\boldsymbol{F}_{s}^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}(U_{i,t}+\delta_{i,t})/n
, then apply Cauchy-Schwartz twice to obtain

\displaystyle g(A_{st})

\displaystyle\leq\left({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{s}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\frac{U_{i,t}+\delta_{i,t}}{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\frac{U_{i,t}}{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\frac{\delta_{i,t}}{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}.

The first term is
\lesssim 1/\sqrt{n}
by Assumption
3
(d) and
2
(c.1); the second is
\lesssim\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}/\sqrt{T}
. Hence
g(A_{st})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}/\sqrt{T}
.

4.

Set
A_{st}=\widetilde{\xi}_{st}:=\boldsymbol{F}_{t}^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}(U_{is}+\delta_{is})/n
, then apply Cauchy-Schwartz twice followed by the maximal inequality to obtain

\displaystyle g(A_{st})

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\|\boldsymbol{F}_{t}\|^{1/2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\frac{U_{is}+\delta_{is}}{n}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{s\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\frac{U_{is}}{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}+\max_{s\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{i}\frac{\delta_{is}}{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}.

The first term in square brackets is
\lesssim 1/\sqrt{n}
by Assumption
3
(d) and
2
(e); the second is
\lesssim\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}/\sqrt{T}
. Hence
g(A_{st})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}/\sqrt{T}
.

Finally, use the identity (
S.3
), the triangle inequality twice and the bounds
(a)
-
(d)
to obtain (
S.4
). Also, by Holder’s inequality

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\|U_{i,t}(\boldsymbol{V}/n)(\boldsymbol{F}_{t}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{F}_{t})\|_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/9}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|U_{i,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\|U_{i,t}(\boldsymbol{V}/n)(\boldsymbol{F}_{t}-\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{F}_{t})\|_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/8}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}.

The first term of (
S.7.4
) is
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}n^{9/p}\left(\frac{\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
due to Lemma
S.11
(a), the result above and the maximal inequality; the second term is
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}n^{4/p}}{T^{1/2-1/p}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}})n^{2/p}
since, from Theorem
1
, we might take
\varrho_{R}=\frac{\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}n^{4/p}}{T^{1/2-1/p}}
in Theorem
2
(b). The last term is
\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}(\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}n^{3/p}/\sqrt{T})n^{4/p}
. Thus

\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\boldsymbol{U}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U})^{\prime}\right\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}\left(\frac{n^{9/p}}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{n^{6/p}}{T^{1/2-1/p}}\right)+\frac{1}{n^{1/2-9/p}}.

(S.5)

The result then follows.
∎

Lemma S.16
.

Let
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{U}
for some non-negative sequence
\varrho_{U}
depending on
n
and
T
, then, for all
h>0
and
\mathcal{D}\subseteq[n]^{2}
,

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\Sigma}-\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\Sigma}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}h[\varrho_{U}(nT)^{3/p}+d^{8/p}/\sqrt{T}]

in the polynomial case; and

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\Sigma}-\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\Sigma}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}h[\varrho_{U}(\psi^{-1}_{p}(nT))^{3}+\psi_{p/4}^{-1}(n^{4})/\sqrt{T}]

in the exponential case, where
d:=|\mathcal{D}|
.

Let
\boldsymbol{i}:=(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathcal{D}^{2}
be a multi-index where
(i_{1},i_{2})\in\mathcal{D}
and
(i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathcal{D}
. Define for
\boldsymbol{i}
and
|\ell|<T
:

\widetilde{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}:=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=|\ell|+1}^{T}U_{i_{1},t}U_{i_{2},t}U_{i_{3},t-|\ell|}U_{i_{4},t-|\ell|};\qquad\gamma_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}:=\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}},

and
\widehat{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}
as
\widetilde{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}
with
U
’s replaced by
\widehat{U}
’s. Also, define

\widetilde{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}:=\sum_{|\ell|<T}k(\ell/h)\widetilde{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}\qquad\upsilon_{\boldsymbol{i}}:=\sum_{|\ell|<T}\gamma_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell},

and
\widehat{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}
as
\widetilde{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}
with
U
’s replaced by
\widehat{U}
’s. Then we write

\displaystyle\widetilde{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}-\upsilon_{\boldsymbol{i}}=\sum_{|\ell|<T}k(\ell/h)(\widetilde{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}-\gamma_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell})+\sum_{|\ell|<T}(k(\ell/h)-1)\gamma_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}.

(S.6)

By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality we have that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|U_{i_{1},t}U_{i_{2},t}U_{i_{3},t-|\ell|}U_{i_{4},t-|\ell|}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{(p+\epsilon)/4}\leq C^{4}
, then by Lemma
S.2
we obtain
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}-\gamma_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}=\lesssim\sqrt{T-|\ell|}/T=\lesssim 1/\sqrt{T}
,
the
\mathcal{L}_{p/4}
-norm of the first term is bounded by

h\sum_{|\ell|<T}|h^{-1}k(\ell/h)|{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}-\gamma_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}\lesssim\left(\frac{h}{\sqrt{T}}\int|k(u)|du\right)=\lesssim h/\sqrt{T},

whereas the second term is deterministic and is shown to be
\lesssim h/\sqrt{T}
by
Andrews 1991
. Thus
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}-\upsilon_{\boldsymbol{i}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/4}=\lesssim h/\sqrt{T})
uniformly in
\boldsymbol{i}\in[n]^{4}
. Thus, union bound followed by Markov’s inequality give us, for
x>0
,

\mathbb{P}(\max_{\boldsymbol{i}}|\widetilde{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}-\upsilon_{\boldsymbol{i}}|\geq x)\leq d^{2}\max_{\boldsymbol{i}}\mathbb{P}(|\widetilde{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}-\upsilon_{\boldsymbol{i}}|\geq x)\lesssim\left(\frac{d^{8/p}h}{x\sqrt{T}}\right)^{p/4}.

(S.7)

We now use the fact that for any
\boldsymbol{x}:=(x_{1},\dots,x_{4})^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and
\boldsymbol{y}:=(y_{1},\dots,y_{4})^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
we have
|\prod_{i=1}^{4}x_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{4}y_{i}|\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{3}\|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}\|_{\infty}^{3-i}\|\boldsymbol{y}\|_{\infty}^{i}
combined with the fact that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}\lesssim 1
to obtain

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{i},\ell}|\widehat{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}-\widetilde{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\boldsymbol{i},t,\ell}|\widehat{U}_{i_{1},t}\widehat{U}_{i_{2},t}\widehat{U}_{i_{3},t-|\ell|}\widehat{U}_{i_{4},t-|\ell|}-U_{i_{1},t}U_{i_{2},t}U_{i_{3},t-|\ell|}U_{i_{4},t-|\ell|}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}^{3})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\{\varrho_{U}[\psi^{-1}(nT)]^{3}\}

Therefore we conclude

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{i}}|\widehat{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}-\widetilde{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\boldsymbol{i},\ell}|\widehat{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}-\widetilde{\gamma}_{\boldsymbol{i}}^{\ell}|\sum_{|\ell|<T}|k(\ell/h)|

(S.8)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left\{h\varrho_{U}[\psi^{-1}(nT)]^{3}\int|k(u)|du\right\}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}h\varrho_{U}[\psi^{-1}(nT)]^{3}.

The result then follows from the triangle inequality
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\|_{\max}\leq\max_{\boldsymbol{i}}|\widehat{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}-\widetilde{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}|+\max_{\boldsymbol{i}}|\widetilde{\upsilon}_{\boldsymbol{i}}-\upsilon_{\boldsymbol{i}}|
, expression (
S.7
) and (
S.8
).
∎

Lemma S.17
.

Let
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{U}
and
\max_{i,j\in[n]}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{\chi}
for some non-negative sequences
\varrho_{U}
and
\varrho_{\chi}
depending on
n
and
T
, then

1.

\max\limits_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{D},t\in[T]}|\widehat{V}_{i,j,t}-V_{i,j,t}|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}(1+\widetilde{s}_{1})\varrho_{U}+\varrho_{\chi}n^{1/p}
;

2.

\max\limits_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widehat{V}_{i,j,t}\widehat{V}_{j,i,t}-V_{i,j,t}V_{i,j,t})\right|\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}(1+\widetilde{s}_{1}+\varrho_{\chi})^{2}\varrho_{UU}+\varrho^{2}_{\chi}n^{4/p}\sqrt{T}
,

where
\widetilde{s}_{1}:=\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}}\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1}
and
\varrho_{UU}
is the rare appearing in Lemma
S.15
.

By the triangle inequality we have, for
i,j\in[n]
and
t\in[T]
,

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{i,j,t}-V_{i,j,t}

\displaystyle=\widehat{U}_{i,t}-U_{i,t}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}^{\prime}\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{-ij,t}+\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t}

\displaystyle=\widehat{U}_{i,t}-U_{i,t}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}^{\prime}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{-ij,t}-\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t})-(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t}

\displaystyle=\widehat{U}_{i,t}-U_{i,t}-\big[\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}+(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j})\big]^{\prime}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{-ij,t}-\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t})-(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t},

Therefore, result (a) follows by Hölder’s and the triangle inequality since

\displaystyle\max_{i,j,t}|\widehat{V}_{i,j,t}-V_{i,j,t}|

\displaystyle\leq(\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1}+\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1})\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{-ij,t}-\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad+|\widehat{U}_{i,t}-U_{i,t}|+\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}}\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1})\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}+\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\big[(1+\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}}\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1})\varrho_{U}+\varrho_{\chi}n^{1/p}\big].

For (b), we write for
i,j\in[n]
and
t\in[T]

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{i,j,t}\widehat{V}_{j,i,t}-V_{i,j,t}V_{i,j,t}

\displaystyle=\widehat{U}_{i,t}^{2}-U_{i,t}^{2}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}^{\prime}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{-ij,t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{-ij,t}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t}\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t}^{\prime})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t}\boldsymbol{U}_{-ij,t}^{\prime}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}).

Then, by Holder’s and the triangle inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}\left|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t\in[T]}(\widehat{V}_{i,j,t}\widehat{V}_{j,i,t}-V_{i,j,t}V_{i,j,t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1+\max_{i,j\in[n]}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}\|_{1})^{2}\|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime})\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{i,j\in[n]}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1}^{2}\|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime})\|_{\max}.

Now,
\max_{i,j\in[n]}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}\|_{1}\leq\max_{i,j\in[n]}\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1}+\max_{i,j\in[n]}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\widetilde{s}_{1}+\varrho_{\chi}
. The order in probability of
