\|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime})\|_{\max}
is given by Lemma
S.15
. By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and Assumption
2
we have
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|U_{i,t}U_{j,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/2}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|U_{i,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|U_{j,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\leq C^{2}
, then by the triangle inequality,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{t\in[T]}U_{i,t}U_{j,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/2}\leq\sum_{t\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|U_{i,t}U_{j,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p/2}\lesssim T
. Finally, by the union bound followed by Markov’s inequality,
\|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime})\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}n^{4/p}\sqrt{T}
. The result follows.
∎

Lemma S.18
.

Let
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}-\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{U}
and
\max_{i,j\in[n]}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\chi}}_{i,j}-\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{1}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\varrho_{\chi}
for some non-negative sequences
\varrho_{U}
and
\varrho_{\chi}
depending on
n
and
T
then

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\Pi}-\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}_{\Pi}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}h\left((1+\widetilde{s}_{1})\varrho_{U}+\varrho_{\chi}n^{1/p}(1+\widetilde{s}_{1})^{3}(nT)^{3/p}+\frac{(1+\widetilde{s}_{2})^{4}n^{8/p}}{\sqrt{T}}\right),

where
\widetilde{s}_{k}:=\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}}\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{k}
for
k\in\{1,2\}
.

The proof is parallel to the proof of Lemma
S.16
, refer to it for details. It suffices to bound in
\max_{i,j,t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|V_{i,j,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p+\epsilon}
,
\max_{i,j,t}|\widehat{V}_{i,j,t}-V_{i,j,T}|
, and
\max_{i,j,t}|V_{i,j,t}|
, where the maximum is over
(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}\subseteq[n]^{2}
and
t\in[T]
. For the first term we have,
\max_{i,j,t}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|V_{i,j,t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p+\epsilon}\leq(1+\max_{i,j}\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{i,j}\|_{2})\sup_{t\in[T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\boldsymbol{U}_{t}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p+\epsilon}\leq(1+M_{2})C
by Assumption
2
. Lemma
S.17
(a) bounds the second term. The last one is upper bounded by
(1+\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}}\|\boldsymbol{\chi}_{ij}\|_{1})\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\max}\lesssim_{\mathbb{P}}\left[(1+M)(nT)^{1/p}\right]
.
∎

S.7.5
Orlicz Norm Results

In this subsection, we show some useful results for Orlicz norms. All these results can be found in
van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
for the case of
\gamma\geq 1
. Recall that for
\gamma\in(0,1)
we define

\psi_{e^{\gamma}}(x):=\mathsf{co}(x\mapsto\exp(x^{\gamma})-1),

where
\mathsf{co}(f)
denote the convex hull of
f
.

Lemma S.19
.

We have

1.

\psi_{e^{\gamma}}(x)=K_{\gamma}x\mathds{1}_{0\leq x<a_{\gamma}}+[\exp(x^{\gamma})-1]\mathds{1}_{x\geq a_{\gamma}}
where
K_{\gamma}:=(\exp a_{\gamma}^{\gamma}-1)/a_{\gamma}
and
a_{\gamma}:=\inf\{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}:x\geq((1-\gamma)/\gamma)^{1/\gamma},K_{\gamma}\leq\gamma\exp(x^{\gamma})/x^{1-\gamma}
. Also
((1-\gamma)/\gamma)^{1/\gamma}\leq a_{\gamma}\leq(1/\gamma)^{1/\gamma}
.

2.

For
p\in[1,\infty)
and
\gamma>0
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{p}\leq C{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}
for some constant
C
depending only on
p
and
\gamma
.

3.

For
0<\gamma_{1}\leq\gamma_{2}
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma_{1}}}\leq C{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma_{2}}}
for constant
C
depending only on
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
.

4.

For random variables
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\max_{j\in[n]}X_{j}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\psi_{e^{\gamma}}}\leq C\psi^{-1}_{e^{\gamma}}(n)\max_{j\in[n]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X_{j}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}
for some constant
C
only depending on
\gamma
.

For part (a), it is straightforward to verify that
x\mapsto\exp(x^{\gamma})-1
is convex on the interval
[((1-\gamma)/\gamma)^{1/\gamma}\lor 0,\infty)
. Also,
\psi_{e^{\gamma}}
is continuous at
z_{\gamma}
(hence continuous on
\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Therefore, convexity follows as the left derivative of
\psi_{e^{\gamma}}
is no larger than the right derivative at
a_{\gamma}
since, by definition,
K_{\gamma}\leq\gamma a_{\gamma}^{\gamma-1}\exp(a^{\gamma}_{\gamma})
.

For part (b), we have
x^{p}=x^{p}1=f(x^{p})+f^{*}(1)
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
f:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{\pm\infty\}
, where
f^{*}
denote the convex conjugate of
f
. Take
f(x)=\psi_{e^{\gamma}}(x^{1/p})
,
x=X(\omega)/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}
(where we assume
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}>0
, otherwise the result is trivial because
X=0
a.s) and take expectation with respect to the law of
X
to obtain

\frac{\mathbb{E}|X|^{p}}{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{p}}\leq\mathbb{E}\psi_{e^{\gamma}}\left(\frac{X}{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}}\right)+f^{*}(1)\leq 1+f^{*}(1),

where
\psi^{*}(1)=\sup_{x\geq 0}\{x-\psi_{e^{\gamma}}(x^{1/p})\}<\infty
and only depends on
p
and
\gamma
, hence we might take
C=(1+\psi^{*}(1))^{1/p}
to conclude.

For (d), we have that
\psi_{e}^{\gamma}
is convex (by part (a)), nondecreasing, nonzero function vanishing at the origin. Note that
x^{\gamma}+y^{\gamma}\leq 2(xy)^{\gamma}
for
x,y\geq 1
, then
\frac{\psi_{e^{\gamma}}(x)\psi_{e^{\gamma}}(y)}{\psi_{e^{\gamma}}(2^{1/\gamma}xy)}\leq\frac{\exp(x^{\gamma}+y^{\gamma})}{\exp(2(xy)^{\gamma})}\leq 1
for
x,y\geq a_{\gamma}\geq 1
. The result then follows from Lemma 2.2.2 in
van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
.

∎

Lemma S.20
.

If
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}<\infty
for
\gamma>0
, there are constants
C_{1}>0
and
C_{2}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}(|X|>x)\leq C_{1}\exp[-(x/C_{2})^{\gamma}]\qquad x>0.

In particular, if
0<{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}<\infty
, we might take
C_{1}=2+\exp\big[(a_{\gamma}/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]\mathds{1}_{0<\gamma<1}
and
C_{2}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}
, where
a_{\gamma}
is defined in Lemma
S.19
(a).

Conversely, if there are constants
C_{1}>0
and
C_{2}>0
such that
\mathbb{P}(|X|>x)\leq C_{1}\exp[-(x/C_{2})^{\gamma}]
for
x>0
, then

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}\leq\begin{cases}C_{2}\big[(1+2C_{1})^{1/\gamma}\lor 2K_{\gamma}C_{1}\Gamma(1/\gamma)/\gamma\big];&0<\gamma<1\\
C_{2}(1+C_{1})^{1/\gamma};&\gamma\geq 1,\end{cases}

where
\Gamma(\cdot)
denotes the Gamma function.

If
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}=0
then
X=0
a.s and the inequality holds for any choice of
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. For the case when
0<{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}<\infty
we have, by Markov inequality and the fact that
x\mapsto\exp\big[(x/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]
is non-decreasing

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|X|\geq x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\exp\big[(|X|/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]\geq\exp\big[(x/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big[-(x/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]\mathbb{E}\exp\big[(|X|/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big].

Using Lemma
S.19
(a), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\exp\big[(|X|/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\exp\big[(|X|/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]\mathds{1}_{|X|<a_{\gamma}}+\mathbb{E}\exp\big[(|X|/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]\mathds{1}_{|X|\geq a_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big[(a_{\gamma}/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]\mathds{1}_{0<\gamma<1}+\mathbb{E}\psi_{e^{\gamma}}(|X|/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})+1

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big[(a_{\gamma}/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}\big]\mathds{1}_{0<\gamma<1}+2.

Combine the last two displays to obtain the first result.

For the converse, we have for
c>0
, by Fubini’s Theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\exp(|X/c|^{\gamma})-1

\displaystyle=\int\int_{0}^{|x|^{\gamma}}c^{-\gamma}\exp(c^{-\gamma}y)dy\mathbb{P}(dx)

\displaystyle=c^{-\gamma}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(|X|\geq x^{1/\gamma})\exp(c^{-\gamma}x)dx.

Since
\mathbb{P}(|X|>x)\leq C_{1}\exp[-(x/C_{2})^{\gamma}]
, for
c>C_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\exp[(|X/c|)^{\gamma}]-1

\displaystyle\leq c^{-\gamma}C_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\left[-x(C_{2}^{-\gamma}-c^{-\gamma})\right]dx\leq\frac{c^{-\gamma}C_{1}}{C_{2}^{-\gamma}-c^{-\gamma}}=\frac{C_{1}}{(c/C_{2})^{\gamma}-1}.

Also

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|X|=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(|X|>x)dx\leq C_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp[-(x/C_{2})^{\gamma}]dx=\tfrac{C_{1}C_{2}}{\gamma}\Gamma(1/\gamma).

Therefore using the last two displays and Lemma
S.19
(a), we have, for
c>C_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\psi_{e^{\gamma}}(|X|/c)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{\gamma}}{c}\mathbb{E}|X|\mathds{1}_{0<\gamma<1}+\mathbb{E}\exp[(|X|/x)^{\gamma}]-1

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{\gamma}C_{1}C_{2}}{c\gamma}\Gamma(1/\gamma)\mathds{1}_{0<\gamma<1}+\frac{C_{1}}{(c/C_{2})^{\gamma}-1}.

For
\gamma\geq 1
the right hand side is less or equal than
1
for
c\geq C_{2}(1+C_{1})^{1/\gamma}
hence
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}\leq C_{2}(1+C_{1})^{1/\gamma}
. For
0<\gamma<1
, the right hand side is less or equal than
1
for
c\geq C_{2}(1+2C_{1})^{1/\gamma}\lor 2K_{\gamma}C_{1}C_{2}\Gamma(1/\gamma)/\gamma
then
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}\leq C_{2}(1+2C_{1})^{1/\gamma}\lor 2K_{\gamma}C_{1}C_{2}\Gamma(1/\gamma)/\gamma
.
∎

Lemma S.21
.

If
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}<\infty
and
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}<\infty
for
\gamma>0
then
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|XY\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma/2}}<\infty
for
\left({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{2}\lor{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{2}\right)
. In particular,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|XY\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma/2}}\leq C\left({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{4}\lor{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{2}\right)
where
C=5^{2/\gamma}
for
\gamma\geq 1
; and
C=(1+2C_{1})^{2/\gamma}\lor 2K_{\gamma/2}C_{0}\Gamma(2/\gamma)/\gamma
with
C_{0}=2(2+\exp\big[(a_{\gamma}/({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}\land{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}))^{\gamma}\big])
for
\gamma\in(0,1)
provided that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}\land{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}>0
.

If
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}=0
or
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}=0
then
XY=0
a.s and the inequality holds trivially. Assume that
0<{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}<\infty
and
0<{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}<\infty
. From Lemma (
S.20
) we have for
x>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|X|>x)\leq C_{X}\exp[-(x/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}]

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|Y|>x)\leq C_{Y}\exp[-(x/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}})^{\gamma}],

for positive constants
C_{X}
and
C_{X}
. Then, by the union bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|XY|\geq x)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(|X|\geq\sqrt{x})+\mathbb{P}(|Y|\geq\sqrt{x})

\displaystyle\leq C_{X}\exp(-x^{\gamma/2}/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{\gamma})+C_{Y}\exp\big[-x^{\gamma/2}/{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{\gamma})

\displaystyle\leq 2(C_{X}\lor C_{Y})\exp\left[-\left(\frac{x}{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|X\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{2}\lor{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|Y\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{e^{\gamma}}^{2}}\right)^{\gamma/2}\right].

Apply once again Lemma (
S.20
) in the other direction to conclude.
∎
